In v0.18 we opt for correctness and simplicity, but longer-term, research into distributed cost-based optimization (how to minimize data shuffling, choosing a distribution key for join, etc.) is needed. For instance, if a query joins two large shards, should we repartition data or send one shard's data to another? Solutions from distributed SQL (like semi-joins, bloom filters to reduce data transfer) could be considered. This is an area for future enhancement once basic federation is in place. - **Consistency vs Performance in Federation:** We rely on Raft for consistency. One consequence is that multi-shard queries see a consistent snapshot cluster-wide (which is good for correctness). However, if a shard fails during a query, how do we handle partial results? We plan to fail the query. In future, perhaps research "partial results resilience" or "mid-query fault tolerance" - but that's complex (Spanner doesn't even attempt that beyond restarting the query). Also, global ORDER BY determinism across shards is solved by merge sort, but if shards have collations or timezone differences for textual/numeric ordering, is that a concern? Likely not if uniform, but worth verifying consistency of sort semantics across shards (should be identical since the engine is same). - **Backup Consistency & Size:** Our backup approach uses a consistent snapshot via Raft leader. One research point: can we do non-blocking cluster-wide snapshot without pausing writes? Possibly using MVCC: take a MVCC snapshot at a timestamp and let writes continue on newer timestamps (like how some DBs do online backup). We simplified by pausing or using Raft snapshot which might briefly pause apply. It might be negligible, but if not, researching more advanced online backup techniques (maybe using incremental log streaming to S3 or using storage-level snapshot like EBS snapshot integrated) is an interesting area for improving RPO/RTO further. Another question is backup compression/deduplication: our plan compresses snapshot, but incremental backup (only send diff pages or WAL since last backup) could hugely save time/storage. We identified it as future work; research into algorithms for efficient graph delta backup could be valuable. - **Cost-based Optimizer Learning:** We set guardrails and calibrations manually for CBO. A possible research direction is using machine learning or adaptive query processing to have the optimizer learn from past mistakes (for example, self-driving DB approaches). That might be overkill for now, but as Geode's workloads diversify, an adaptive component could auto-tune the cost model (especially for graph-specific patterns where relational assumptions might not hold well). Additionally, extending the CBO to consider new things like parallel plans or multi-shard plans will require new research; e.g., how to cost network IO vs local IO, how to cost multi-thread CPU usage (maybe treat cost as total work, then evaluate that partial can be parallelized). - **CDC Improvements beyond Headers:** We handled custom headers and retries, but one open question: how to ensure exactly-once delivery semantics end-to-end? We provided idempotency keys for consumers, but on Geode side, we still do at-least-once. Could we track acknowledgments from a webhook to avoid even duplicates being sent? That would lean towards a more complex protocol (two-phase commit with the sink - often not worth it). But maybe research integration with reliable event systems (like writing to Kafka as intermediate, or implementing an outbox pattern) might interest some users. We have Kafka sink already, which covers exactly-once via that ecosystem if needed. - **Security enhancements:** After TDE and MFA, what's next? Possibly **row-level security (RLS)** policies or **data masking** for sensitive fields. We saw mention of roles and RLS enforcement in v0.17 DML features[\[98\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L20-L23), so maybe that's done. But things like **audit logging** of queries (who accessed what data) might be required in some settings. Another is **key rotation automation:** currently not fully solved in plan, so research into low-impact re-encryption strategies would be useful (for example, using many small encryption units to allow rolling key rotation gradually without massive rewrites). - **Full-Text Search beyond basics:** If Geode users require more advanced search, integration with an existing search engine library (like Lucene) could be considered instead of reinventing all features. We added phrase and highlight, but Lucene has decades of optimization (for fuzzy search, wildcard, multi-language analyzers, etc.). Perhaps research feasibility of plugging Lucene or Tantivy (Rust) as an index provider for Geode. That involves JNI or FFI overhead but could leapfrog capabilities. Alternatively, see how far we go in pure Zig - maybe it's sufficient for moderate usage. Multi-language support might eventually require either implementing stemming dictionaries or linking to ICU library for Unicode analysis - we flagged that as future; it's a potential gap if someone needs, say, Japanese text search - our system will work (by indexing each character or word if separated) but not as well as a specialized analyzer would. - **Multi-tenancy and workload management:** As concurrency and distributed usage grow, research into workload management will matter. E.g., if one user runs a huge expensive query, should we throttle it to not consume all threads? We currently rely on OS fairness. In future, a proper scheduler with query priorities or resource groups might be needed (especially if Geode is offered as a managed service with multi-tenant usage). We have no explicit plan for that yet - an area for research/experimentation once basic concurrency is settled. - **Graph-specific optimizations:** Many of our improvements borrow from relational DB and search engine world. There may be graph-specific enhancements worth exploring. For example, improving the CBO to be aware of typical graph pattern costs (like the "expand" or traversal operations could use selectivity estimations based on degree distributions). Or introducing multi-threaded graph algorithms (which the plan hints at with GPU, but even CPU multi-thread for BFS, etc.). Research from academia on parallel graph processing (e.g., GraphX, Ligra) could be tapped to make Geode's built-in algorithms faster using multi-core. We have it on roadmap (GPU, etc.) but need careful integration with transactional engine. - **Testing gaps:** Our plan outlines extensive testing, but one gap could be formal verification of isolation (like using tools to verify serializability or using Jepsen tests for correctness under concurrency and failover). It might be worth investing in Jepsen or similar framework testing for Geode, especially around distributed consistency and transaction isolation, to catch edge cases not covered by unit tests. That goes beyond what we described but is a wise step for truly robust confidence. - **Feature interaction corner cases:** We should keep an eye on how these new features interact. For instance, a distributed query that triggers full-text search on shards - will that work seamlessly with our design (likely yes, we do local FTS on each shard and merge results with global ranking? We didn't explicitly detail that, but presumably treat it like any other filter/score). Or running backup during heavy concurrent transactions - can the system handle that safely (maybe slight pause, but should not crash). We will test some combos (like backup while queries running, see if snapshot is consistent). Multi-threading plus TDE plus distributed queries plus FTS - all combined should still operate (we believe so because they address orthogonal concerns, but integration testing covering combinations is needed to confirm no unforeseen interference, e.g., multi-threading could reveal a race in encryption code if not careful).

Each of these open questions can feed into the next planning cycles. They aren't critical for initial adoption, but addressing them will become important as Geode scales to more diverse and demanding scenarios. It's encouraging that none of the identified gaps from v0.17 were fundamental show-stoppers; they were all features that other systems have solved. By tackling them with known techniques, we clear the path to then innovate on more graph-specific or novel aspects, guided by future research and user feedback.

### A3. Glossary

- **ACID**: Set of database transaction properties - Atomicity, Consistency, Isolation, Durability. Ensures reliable transactions.
- **BM25**: A ranking algorithm for full-text search (Best Matching 25), uses term frequency-inverse document frequency with length normalization to score document relevance.
- **CANARY tests**: Geode's internal evidence tagging system linking requirements to tests[\[16\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/kb/reviews/2025-09-24-Review.md#L4-L12). Ensures features are implemented and tested ("canary" markers).
- **CDC**: Change Data Capture. In Geode, capturing changes (inserts/updates/deletes) in the database and delivering them to external systems (sinks) like Kafka or webhooks[\[54\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L32-L40).
- **CBO**: Cost-Based Optimizer. The query planner that chooses query execution plans based on estimated costs (using statistics) rather than fixed rules. Geode's CBO v1 was optional in v0.17[\[20\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L25-L31).
- **DLQ**: Dead Letter Queue. A storage for messages (or CDC events) that could not be delivered after retries[\[58\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L34-L38). Allows analysis or later reprocessing of failed events.
- **Federation (Distributed Query Federation)**: The ability to execute a query across multiple data partitions or shards and combine the results as if on a single dataset[\[3\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/kb/reviews/2025-09-21-Review.md#L37-L40). In Geode's context, running GQL queries across multiple Geode instances (shards).
- **FTS**: Full-Text Search. Indexing and querying text content within attributes. Geode supports basic keyword search, and we plan to add phrases, highlighting, etc.[\[10\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/kb/reviews/2025-09-21-Review.md#L34-L39).
- **MFA**: Multi-Factor Authentication. Requiring an additional verification factor (like a one-time code or push approval) besides password for login[\[9\]](https://fuzziebrain.com/content/enable-mfa-for-oracle-databases#:~:text=The%20recent%20Oracle%20Database%2023ai,has%20been%20backported%20to%2019c). Enhances account security.
- **MVCC**: Multi-Version Concurrency Control. A concurrency control method where readers see a snapshot (a version) of the database, and writers create new versions, enabling snapshot isolation. Geode uses MVCC for transactions (snapshot isolation is implied in review)[\[95\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/kb/reviews/2025-09-24-Review.md#L18-L20).
- **QUIC**: A modern transport protocol (Quick UDP Internet Connections) that Geode uses instead of TCP for client-server communication, always encrypted with TLS[\[18\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L61-L69). Replaced TCP in v0.17.
- **Raft**: A consensus algorithm/protocol used in Geode for cluster coordination and replication (achieves consistency across nodes). Ensures a single leader applies log of transactions, etc.[\[15\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L40-L48). Used for durability and replication of writes.
- **RLS (Row-Level Security)**: A security mechanism to restrict access to specific rows in a table/graph based on user roles or policies[\[98\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L20-L23). Geode enforces RLS on DML as of v0.17.
- **Sharding**: Partitioning data into multiple pieces (shards) each managed by a separate server to scale horizontally. Geode currently doesn't support query sharding yet (only replication), which federation will address[\[3\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/kb/reviews/2025-09-21-Review.md#L37-L40).
- **Snapshot (DB Snapshot)**: A consistent point-in-time copy of the database state. Used for backups or distributed consistency (Raft snapshot is used to bring new nodes up to speed quickly)[\[15\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L40-L48).
- **Stopwords**: Common words that are often filtered out in full-text indexing (e.g., "the", "and") to reduce noise. We consider whether to handle these in Geode's FTS (not currently, might in future).
- **TDE**: Transparent Data Encryption. Encrypting database files on disk so that data is protected at rest. Transparent means the DB engine does encryption/decryption automatically, not requiring query changes[\[92\]](https://www.enterprisedb.com/blog/everything-need-know-postgres-data-encryption#:~:text=protecting%20data%20at%20rest,requirements%20such%20as%20PCI%20DSS).
- **Tie-break (Ordering)**: A deterministic rule to order results that are otherwise equal in sort key. Geode v0.17 implemented tie-break rules UA005/UA006 for consistent query results ordering[\[34\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L14-L17).
- **Top-K**: In context of queries, retrieving the top K results (like LIMIT K) possibly from distributed shards. A distributed top-K algorithm finds the K highest values across partitions efficiently[\[30\]](https://www.yugabyte.com/blog/optimize-pagination-distributed-data-maintain-ordering/#:~:text=0%20and%20event_ts%20,infinity%27%20ORDER%20BY%20event_ts%20DESC).
- **TTL**: Time-To-Live, not explicitly mentioned but common in data; irrelevant here except possibly for backup retention (though we handle retention via S3 lifecycle rather than TTL).
- **2PC**: Two-Phase Commit. A protocol for distributed transactions to ensure atomic commit across participants. Geode has "2PC over Raft" for cross-shard transactions (coordinator logs the outcome)[\[15\]](https://github.com/spyderorg/geode/blob/1fda273b48808cc34f507c838b87ddd783dc8f74/docs/v0.17.0-RELEASE-NOTES.md#L40-L48). Used to commit or abort changes on all shards consistently.
